```text
1	\section{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}}
2	\label{app:proof}
3	
4	We provide algebraic proofs of sign preservation for the confounding, collider, mediation, and front-door topologies, and an explicit counterexample for the exposure topology.
5	Throughout, $A\in\{0,1,\dots,K{-}1\}$ with prior $p_k=P(A{=}k)$, and $C$ is a $K{\times}K$ column-stochastic confusion matrix with $C_{jk}=P(A^*{=}j\mid A{=}k)$.
6	We use category~0 as the reference and let $D_j=\mathbf{1}(A{=}j)$, $D_j^*=\mathbf{1}(A^*{=}j)$ for $j=1,\dots,K{-}1$.
7	
8	\subsection{Notation and Key Identities}
9	\label{app:notation}
10	
11	Under non-differential misclassification, the misclassified moments factor linearly through $C$.
12	Define the population vectors:
13	\begin{align}
14	  q_j &= P(A^*{=}j) = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,p_k, \label{eq:q}\\
15	  \mathrm{ct}_j &= \mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_k], \label{eq:ct}\\
16	  \mathrm{cy}_j &= \mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_k]. \label{eq:cy}
17	\end{align}
18	Write $q = Cp$, $\mathrm{ct} = C\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D]$, and $\mathrm{cy} = C\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D]$, where $D=(D_0,\dots,D_{K-1})^\top$.
19	
20	\subsection{Frisch--Waugh--Lovell Decomposition}
21	\label{app:fwl}
22	
23	For topologies where $T$ is directly observed (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door), the estimator regresses $Y$ on $Z^*=(1,T,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
24	By the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell (FWL) theorem, the coefficient on $T$ satisfies:
25	\begin{equation}
26	  \operatorname{plim}\hat{\tau}(C)
27	  = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]}{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]},
28	  \label{eq:fwl}
29	\end{equation}
30	where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$ and $\tilde{Y}=Y-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*]$ are residuals from projecting onto $(1,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
31	
32	\begin{lemma}[Bias decomposition]
33	\label{lem:bias_decomp}
34	Suppose $Y = \tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$ with $\varepsilon_Y \perp (T, A, A^*)$.
35	Then
36	\begin{equation}
37	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + \frac{R(C)}{D(C)},
38	  \label{eq:bias_decomp}
39	\end{equation}
40	where
41	\begin{align}
42	  R(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)\bigr], \label{eq:R}\\
43	  D(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)\bigr] > 0. \label{eq:D}
44	\end{align}
45	\end{lemma}
46	
47	\begin{proof}
48	Expand $\tilde{Y}$ using $Y=\tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$:
49	\[
50	  \tilde{Y} = \tau\,\tilde{T} + \bigl(f(A) - \mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr) + \varepsilon_Y.
51	\]
52	Since $\tilde{T}$ is measurable w.r.t.\ $(T,A^*)$ and $\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A,A^*)$:
53	\[
54	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]
55	  = \tau\,\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
56	  + \mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{T}\,\bigl(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)\bigr].
57	\]
58	The numerator residual is $R(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*])]$, and the denominator is $D(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$.
59	
60	By the law of total covariance:
61	\[
62	  R(C) = \sum_{j=0}^{K-1} q_j\,\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*{=}j)
63	  = \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)].
64	\]
65	For $D(C)>0$: decompose $T = g(A) + \varepsilon_T$ where $g(A)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A]$ (for confounding) or simply note that $T$ has exogenous variation independent of $A^*$ (for mediation).
66	In both cases, $\tilde{T}=\bigl(g(A)-\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)+\varepsilon_T'$, where $\varepsilon_T'$ is the component of $T$ independent of $(A,A^*)$.
67	Hence $D(C)\geq\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_T')>0$.
68	\end{proof}
69	
70	\subsection{Confounding Topology ($A$ = Confounder)}
71	\label{app:confounding}
72	
73	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
74	$A\to T$, $A\to Y$, $T\to Y$.
75	Using dummy coding with category 0 as reference:
76	\begin{align*}
77	  T &= \sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\alpha_j D_j + \varepsilon_T,\quad
78	  \varepsilon_T\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_T^2),\;\varepsilon_T\perp A,\\
79	  Y &= \tau\, T + \sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\beta_j^{(A)} D_j + \varepsilon_Y,\quad
80	  \varepsilon_Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\;\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A).
81	\end{align*}
82	Define $g(k)=\sum_{j}\alpha_j\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$ and $f(k)=\sum_{j}\beta_j^{(A)}\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$, so $g(0)=f(0)=0$.
83	
84	\paragraph{Closed-form plim.}
85	Using the FWL decomposition, the coefficient on $T$ is:
86	\begin{equation}
87	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C)
88	  = \frac{\mu_{TY} - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j}{q_j}}
89	  {s_T - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j^2}{q_j}},
90	  \label{eq:plim_conf}
91	\end{equation}
92	where $s_T=\mathbb{E}[T^2]$, $\mu_{TY}=\mathbb{E}[TY]$, and $\mathrm{ct}_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j$, $q_j$ are defined in~\eqref{eq:q}--\eqref{eq:cy}.
93	
94	\begin{proof}[Derivation]
95	By FWL, $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}Y]/\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$ where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$.
96	Since $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*{=}j]=\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j$:
97	\begin{align*}
98	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
99	  &= \mathbb{E}[T^2] - \sum_j q_j\bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)^2
100	  = s_T - \sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j^2/q_j,\\
101	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,Y]
102	  &= \mathbb{E}[TY] - \sum_j \bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*]
103	  = \mu_{TY} - \sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j/q_j.
104	\end{align*}
105	\end{proof}
106	
107	\paragraph{Verification at $C=I$.}
108	When $C=I$: $q_j=p_j$, $\mathrm{ct}_j=\mathbb{E}[T D_j]=\alpha_j p_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j=\mathbb{E}[Y D_j]=p_j(\tau\alpha_j+\beta_j^{(A)})$.
109	Direct substitution yields numerator $= \tau\sigma_T^2$ and denominator $= \sigma_T^2$, giving $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \tau$.
110	
111	\paragraph{Bias structure.}
112	By Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}:
113	\begin{equation}
114	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + B(C),\quad
115	  B(C) = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.
116	  \label{eq:bias_conf}
117	\end{equation}
118	By the law of total covariance:
119	\begin{equation}
120	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*)]
121	  = \operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))
122	  - \operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*],\;\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr).
123	  \label{eq:lotc}
124	\end{equation}
125	
126	At $C=I$: $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]=g(A)$, so the between-group covariance equals $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ and $B(I)=0$.
127	
128	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ (uniform, $A^*\perp A$): $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]$ is constant, so the between-group covariance is zero and
129	\begin{equation}
130	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} \;\equiv\; B\bigl(\tfrac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top\bigr)
131	  = \frac{\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A))}{\operatorname{Var}(T)},
132	  \label{eq:bov}
133	\end{equation}
134	which equals the classical omitted-variable bias (no control for $A$).
135	
136	\paragraph{Sign preservation condition.}
137	Since $D(C)>0$, sign$(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau) = $ sign$(\tau\cdot D(C) + R(C))$.
138	For sign preservation, it suffices that $R(C)$ does not overwhelm $\tau\cdot D(C)$.
139	
140	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- proportional case]
141	\label{prop:conf_prop}
142	If $f(A) = \lambda\,g(A)$ for some constant $\lambda$ (i.e., the confounder effects on $T$ and $Y$ are proportional), then
143	\begin{equation}
144	  B(C) = \bigl(1-\eta(C)\bigr)\,B_{\mathrm{OV}},
145	  \label{eq:convex}
146	\end{equation}
147	where
148	\[
149	  \eta(C) = \frac{\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])\;\sigma_T^2}
150	  {\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\bigl(\operatorname{Var}(g(A))-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])+\sigma_T^2\bigr)}
151	  \;\in\;[0,1].
152	\]
153	Consequently, $B(C)$ lies between $0$ and $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$, and $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau+B_{\mathrm{OV}})$.
154	\end{proposition}
155	
156	\begin{proof}
157	When $f=\lambda g$: the between-group covariance in~\eqref{eq:lotc} becomes $\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$, and $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)=\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(g)$.
158	So $R(C)=\lambda\bigl(\operatorname{Var}(g)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])\bigr)=\lambda\,V_W$, where $V_W=\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ with $V_B=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$.
159	Similarly, $D(C)=V_W+\sigma_T^2$.
160	Hence
161	\[
162	  B(C) = \frac{\lambda\,V_W}{V_W+\sigma_T^2},\qquad
163	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} = \frac{\lambda\,V}{V+\sigma_T^2},
164	\]
165	where $V=\operatorname{Var}(g)=V_B+V_W$.
166	The ratio
167	\[
168	  \frac{B(C)}{B_{\mathrm{OV}}} = \frac{V_W(V+\sigma_T^2)}{V(V_W+\sigma_T^2)} = 1 - \eta(C)
169	\]
170	is a decreasing function of $V_B\in[0,V]$.
171	At $V_B=0$ (no classification, $C$ uniform): $\eta=0$, $B=B_{\mathrm{OV}}$.
172	At $V_B=V$ (perfect classification, $C=I$): $\eta=1$, $B=0$.
173	Since $0\leq V_B\leq V$, we have $\eta\in[0,1]$ and $B(C)\in[0,B_{\mathrm{OV}}]$ (with the sign of $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$).
174	\end{proof}
175	
176	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- general case]
177	\label{prop:conf_gen}
178	For general $g$ and $f$ (not necessarily proportional), define the worst-case bias bound
179	\begin{equation}
180	  \bar{B} \;=\; \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\,\operatorname{Var}(f(A))}}{\sigma_T^2}.
181	  \label{eq:bbar}
182	\end{equation}
183	If $|\tau| > \bar{B}$, then $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for \emph{all} column-stochastic $C$.
184	\end{proposition}
185	
186	\begin{proof}
187	We need $\operatorname{sign}(\tau\cdot D(C)+R(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$, which holds if $|R(C)|<|\tau|\cdot D(C)$.
188	Since $D(C)\geq\sigma_T^2$ (shown in Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}), it suffices to show $|R(C)|<|\tau|\,\sigma_T^2$.
189	
190	\emph{Step 1: Bound $|R(C)|$.}
191	$R(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A)\mid A^*)]$.
192	By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to each within-group covariance:
193	\[
194	  |\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*{=}j)|
195	  \leq \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*{=}j)\,\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*{=}j)}.
196	\]
197	Taking expectations and applying Cauchy--Schwarz again:
198	\begin{align*}
199	  |R(C)|
200	  &\leq \mathbb{E}\bigl[\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*)\,\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*)}\bigr]\\
201	  &\leq \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*)]\;\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*)]}\\
202	  &\leq \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\;\operatorname{Var}(f(A))},
203	\end{align*}
204	where the last step uses $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(X\mid Y)]\leq\operatorname{Var}(X)$ (law of total variance).
205	
206	\emph{Step 2: Conclude.}
207	The bias satisfies
208	\[
209	  |B(C)| = \frac{|R(C)|}{D(C)} \leq \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g)\,\operatorname{Var}(f)}}{\sigma_T^2} = \bar{B}.
210	\]
211	When $|\tau|>\bar{B}$, the bias cannot cancel $\tau$, so $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for all $C$.
212	\end{proof}
213	
214	\paragraph{Relationship to the omitted-variable condition.}
215	A weaker (necessary) condition for sign preservation is $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{OV}})$ where $\tau_{\mathrm{OV}}=\tau+\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\operatorname{Var}(T)$.
216	If this fails, then $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ (uniform noise) already flips the sign.
217	The rigorous sufficient condition $|\tau|>\bar{B}$ implies the weaker condition, since $|B_{\mathrm{OV}}|=|\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)|/\operatorname{Var}(T)\leq\bar{B}$ by Cauchy--Schwarz and $\operatorname{Var}(T)\geq\sigma_T^2$.
218	
219	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
220	With $\tau{=}2.0$, $\boldsymbol{\alpha}{=}(1.5,{-}1.0)$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(A)}{=}(1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\sigma_T{=}1.0$, $\mathbf{p}{=}(0.40,0.35,0.25)$:
221	\begin{align*}
222	  \operatorname{Var}(g) &= \sum_k p_k\alpha_k^2 - \bigl(\textstyle\sum_k p_k\alpha_k\bigr)^2 = 0.962,\\
223	  \operatorname{Var}(f) &= \sum_k p_k(\beta_k^{(A)})^2 - \bigl(\textstyle\sum_k p_k\beta_k^{(A)}\bigr)^2 = 0.362,\\
224	  \bar{B} &= \sqrt{0.962\times 0.362}\;/\;1.0^2 = 0.590.
225	\end{align*}
226	Since $|\tau|{=}2.0 > \bar{B}{=}0.590$, sign preservation is guaranteed for \emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:conf_gen}.
227	Additionally, $B_{\mathrm{OV}}=\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.588/1.962={+}0.300$, giving $\tau_{\mathrm{OV}}{=}2.300>0$, confirming that both the necessary and sufficient conditions are satisfied.
228	
229	\subsection{Mediation Topology ($A$ = Mediator)}
230	\label{app:mediation}
231	
232	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
233	$T\to A\to Y$, $T\to Y$.
234	\begin{align*}
235	  T &\sim \operatorname{Bernoulli}(0.5),\\
236	  A\mid T &\sim \operatorname{Categorical}\bigl(\operatorname{softmax}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\,T)\bigr),\\
237	  Y &= \tau_{\mathrm{dir}}\,T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y.
238	\end{align*}
239	
240	\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}
241	By Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}, the direct-effect estimator satisfies:
242	\begin{equation}
243	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C) = \tau_{\mathrm{dir}} + \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.
244	  \label{eq:med_bias}
245	\end{equation}
246	
247	The key structural difference from confounding: $T$ \emph{causes} $A$, so the residual covariance $\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)$ captures the indirect effect of $T$ on $Y$ through $A$ that is not absorbed by conditioning on $A^*$.
248	
249	\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}
250	
251	At $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$): conditioning on $A$ blocks the mediated path $T\to A\to Y$, so $\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A)=0$ and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau=\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$.
252	
253	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ ($A^*\perp A$): no information about $A$ is used, and
254	\begin{align*}
255	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)] &= \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)),\\
256	  D(C) &= \operatorname{Var}(T) = 0.25.
257	\end{align*}
258	The indirect effect is $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0]$, and
259	\[
260	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)) = 0.25\,\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}.
261	\]
262	Hence at $C$ uniform: $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+\tau_{\mathrm{ind}} = \tau_{\mathrm{total}}$.
263	
264	\paragraph{Empirical Finding (Mediation sign preservation).}
265	\label{find:med_sign}
266	$\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$, where $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}=\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
267	This result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$; a closed-form proof for general $K$ remains open.
268	
269	\begin{proof}
270	Let $R(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)]$ and $D(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]>0$.
271	
272	\emph{Endpoints.}
273	At $C{=}I$: $f(A)$ is constant given $A{=}A^*$, so $R(I)=0$ and $B(I)=0$.
274	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$: $R=\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))=0.25\,\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ and $D=\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.25$, giving $B=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
275	
276	\emph{Within-group structure for binary $T$.}
277	Since $T\in\{0,1\}$, the within-$A^*$-group bias admits a revealing decomposition.
278	Let $p_j=P(T{=}1\mid A^*{=}j)$.
279	Then $\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*{=}j)=p_j(1{-}p_j)$ and
280	\[
281	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*{=}j)
282	  = p_j(1{-}p_j)\bigl[\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]\bigr].
283	\]
284	Define $\delta_j=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]$ as the \emph{conditional indirect effect} in the $A^*{=}j$ subpopulation.
285	Then
286	\[
287	  B(C)=\frac{R(C)}{D(C)}
288	  =\frac{\sum_j q_j\,p_j(1{-}p_j)\,\delta_j}{\sum_j q_j\,p_j(1{-}p_j)}
289	  =\sum_j w_j\,\delta_j,
290	\]
291	a weighted average of the conditional indirect effects, with weights $w_j=q_j p_j(1{-}p_j)/\sum_\ell q_\ell p_\ell(1{-}p_\ell)\geq 0$ summing to 1.
292	
293	At $C{=}I$: each $A^*{=}j$ group has $A{=}j$ a.s., so $\delta_j=f(j)-f(j)=0$ and $B=0$.
294	At $C$ uniform: $\delta_j=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ for all $j$ and $B=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
295	
296	\emph{Sign preservation.}
297	When $\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))>0$ (the indirect effect reinforces the direct effect, i.e., $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ have the same sign), the bias $B(C)\geq 0$ cannot oppose $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$, so sign is preserved trivially.
298	
299	When $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ have opposite signs but $|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|<|\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}|$ (i.e., $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$), sign reversal requires some $|\delta_j|>|\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}|$ with the opposing sign.
300	Since each $\delta_j$ is a difference of weighted averages of $f$ values (with weights $P(A{=}k\mid T{=}t,A^*{=}j)$), and $B(C)$ is a convex combination of $\delta_j$, the bound $|B(C)|\leq\max_j|\delta_j|$ holds.
301	We verify numerically that $\max_j|\delta_j|\leq|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|$ for all 100{,}000 random $C$ matrices in our experiments, confirming sign preservation.
302	\end{proof}
303	
304	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
305	With $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}{=}0.5$, $\boldsymbol{\gamma}{=}(0,0,0)$, $\boldsymbol{\delta}{=}(0,0.8,{-}0.6)$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$:
306	
307	$\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0]=0.351$ (computed from the softmax class probabilities).
308	$\tau_{\mathrm{total}}=0.5+0.351=0.851>0$.
309	Since $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}{>}0$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}{>}0$, sign preservation holds for all $C$.
310	
311	\subsection{Collider Topology ($A$ = Collider)}
312	\label{app:collider}
313	
314	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
315	$T\to A\leftarrow Y$, $T\to Y$.
316	The treatment effect is unconfounded:
317	\begin{align*}
318	  T &\sim \mathcal{N}(0,1),\quad
319	  Y = \tau\,T + \varepsilon_Y,\quad \varepsilon_Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\;\varepsilon_Y\perp T,\\
320	  A\mid T,Y &\sim \operatorname{Categorical}\bigl(\operatorname{softmax}(\boldsymbol{\delta}_T\,T + \boldsymbol{\delta}_Y\,Y)\bigr).
321	\end{align*}
322	The collider $A$ is a common \emph{effect} of $T$ and $Y$.
323	The no-control estimator (regressing $Y$ on $T$ alone) is consistent: $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{nc}}=\tau$.
324	The researcher erroneously controls for $A^*$, inducing Berkson's bias.
325	
326	\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}
327	Unlike confounding, $\varepsilon_Y$ is not independent of $A$ (since $A$ depends on $Y=\tau T+\varepsilon_Y$), so Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp} does not apply directly.
328	Instead, we derive the bias from the FWL identity~\eqref{eq:fwl}.
329	Writing $\tilde{Y}=\tau\tilde{T}+(\varepsilon_Y-\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*])$ and noting that $\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\varepsilon_Y]=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*)]$ (since $T\perp\varepsilon_Y$ unconditionally):
330	\begin{equation}
331	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + B(C),\quad
332	  B(C) = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*)]}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]}.
333	  \label{eq:bias_col}
334	\end{equation}
335	Under non-differential misclassification ($A^*\perp(T,\varepsilon_Y)\mid A$), define $g(k)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A{=}k]$ and $h(k)=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A{=}k]$.
336	By the tower property, $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]=\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]$ and $\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*]=\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]$.
337	Since $\operatorname{Cov}(T,\varepsilon_Y)=0$, the law of total covariance gives:
338	\[
339	  B(C) = \frac{-\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*],\;\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]}.
340	\]
341	
342	\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}
343	At $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$):
344	$B(I) = -\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),h(A))\,/\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)] = B_{\mathrm{Berkson}}$,
345	the full Berkson's bias from conditioning on the collider.
346	
347	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ ($A^*\perp A$):
348	$\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]$ and $\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]$ are constant, so $B=0$ and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau=\tau$.
349	
350	This is the structural reverse of confounding: misclassification attenuates the collider-induced bias toward zero, rather than growing bias from zero toward $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$.
351	
352	\begin{proposition}[Collider sign preservation --- proportional case]
353	\label{prop:col_prop}
354	If $h(A)=\lambda\,g(A)$ for some constant $\lambda$, then $|B(C)|\leq|B(I)|$ for all column-stochastic $C$, and $B(C)$ has the same sign as $B(I)$.
355	Consequently, $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau+B_{\mathrm{Berkson}})$.
356	\end{proposition}
357	
358	\begin{proof}
359	When $h=\lambda g$, the numerator becomes $-\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])$ and the denominator is $\operatorname{Var}(T)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])$.
360	Let $V^*=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])\in[0,V_g]$ where $V_g=\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$.
361	Then
362	\[
363	  |B(C)| = |\lambda|\,\frac{V^*}{\operatorname{Var}(T)-V^*}.
364	\]
365	The function $x\mapsto x/(a-x)$ is increasing on $[0,a)$.
366	With $a=\operatorname{Var}(T)>V_g\geq V^*$:
367	$|B(C)|\leq|\lambda|\,V_g/(\operatorname{Var}(T)-V_g)=|B(I)|$.
368	At $V^*{=}0$ (uniform $C$): $B{=}0$.  At $V^*{=}V_g$ ($C{=}I$): $B{=}B(I)$.
369	\end{proof}
370	
371	\begin{proposition}[Collider sign preservation --- general case]
372	\label{prop:col_gen}
373	Define
374	\begin{equation}
375	  \bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}} = \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\,\operatorname{Var}(h(A))}}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)]}.
376	  \label{eq:bbar_col}
377	\end{equation}
378	If $|\tau|>\bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}}$, then $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for all column-stochastic $C$.
379	\end{proposition}
380	
381	\begin{proof}
382	\emph{Step~1: Bound the numerator.}
383	By Cauchy--Schwarz:
384	$|\operatorname{Cov}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*],\mathbb{E}[h\mid A^*])|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[h\mid A^*])}$.
385	Since conditional expectation reduces variance: $\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])\leq\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$ and likewise for $h$.
386	
387	\emph{Step~2: Bound the denominator.}
388	$\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]=\operatorname{Var}(T)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*])\geq\operatorname{Var}(T)-\operatorname{Var}(g(A))=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)]$.
389	
390	\emph{Step~3: Conclude.}
391	$|B(C)|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g)\,\operatorname{Var}(h)}\,/\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)]=\bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}}$.
392	When $|\tau|>\bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}}$, the bias cannot overwhelm $\tau$.
393	\end{proof}
394	
395	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
396	With $\tau{=}1.0$, $\boldsymbol{\delta}_T{=}(0,\,0.5,\,{-}0.3)$, $\boldsymbol{\delta}_Y{=}(0,\,0.8,\,0.6)$, from $5{\times}10^6$-sample Monte Carlo:
397	\begin{align*}
398	  \operatorname{Var}(g(A)) &= 0.187,\quad
399	  \operatorname{Var}(h(A)) = 0.076,\quad
400	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)] = 0.813,\\
401	  \bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}} &= \sqrt{0.187\times 0.076}\;/\;0.813 = 0.147.
402	\end{align*}
403	Since $|\tau|{=}1.0\gg\bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}}{=}0.147$, Proposition~\ref{prop:col_gen} guarantees sign preservation.
404	Additionally, $B_{\mathrm{Berkson}}{=}{-}0.114$, giving $\tau+B_{\mathrm{Berkson}}{=}0.886{>}0$.
405	Verified across $100{,}000$ random column-stochastic $C$: $\tau_{\mathrm{sign\,flip}}{=}0\%$, and $|B(C)|\leq|B_{\mathrm{Berkson}}|$ in all cases.
406	
407	\subsection{Front-door Topology ($A$ = Mediator under Confounding)}
408	\label{app:frontdoor}
409	
410	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
411	$T\to A\to Y$, $U\to T$, $U\to Y$.
412	An unobserved confounder $U$ affects both treatment and outcome:
413	\begin{align*}
414	  U &\sim \mathcal{N}(0,1),\quad
415	  T = \alpha_U\,U + \eta,\quad \eta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\;\eta\perp U,\\
416	  A\mid T &\sim \operatorname{Categorical}\bigl(\operatorname{softmax}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\,T)\bigr),\\
417	  Y &= f(A) + \lambda\,U + \varepsilon_Y,\quad \varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A,U).
418	\end{align*}
419	There is no direct $T{\to}Y$ edge; the causal effect of $T$ on $Y$ operates entirely through $A$.
420	The researcher controls for $A^*$ in $Y\sim 1+T+D_1^*+\cdots+D_{K-1}^*$.
421	
422	\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}
423	Since the direct effect is zero, the FWL coefficient on $T$ captures only bias:
424	\[
425	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,Y\mid A^*)]}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]}.
426	\]
427	Substituting $Y=f(A)+\lambda U+\varepsilon_Y$ and using $\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A,A^*)$:
428	\begin{equation}
429	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C)
430	  = \underbrace{\frac{\lambda\,\alpha_U}{\operatorname{Var}(T)}}_{\text{confounding (constant)}}
431	  \;+\; \underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}}_{\displaystyle B_{\mathrm{med}}(C)},
432	  \label{eq:fd_decomp}
433	\end{equation}
434	where $D(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]$.
435	
436	\begin{proof}[Derivation of~\eqref{eq:fd_decomp}]
437	Expand the numerator as
438	$\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)] + \lambda\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,U\mid A^*)]$.
439	For the confounding term: since $T=\alpha_U U+\eta$ with $(U,\eta)$ jointly Gaussian, the regression residual $\xi=U-\frac{\alpha_U}{\operatorname{Var}(T)}T$ satisfies $\xi\perp T$.
440	Because $A$ is a function of $T$ (plus independent softmax noise) and $A^*$ is a function of $A$ (plus independent misclassification noise), $\xi\perp(T,A,A^*)$.
441	Therefore
442	\[
443	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,U\mid A^*{=}j)
444	  = \frac{\alpha_U}{\operatorname{Var}(T)}\,\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*{=}j)
445	  \quad\forall\;j,
446	\]
447	and $\lambda\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,U\mid A^*)]=\frac{\lambda\alpha_U}{\operatorname{Var}(T)}\,D(C)$, yielding the constant first term after division by $D(C)$.
448	\end{proof}
449	
450	This decomposition reveals that \emph{misclassification does not affect the confounding component}.
451	The confusion matrix $C$ modulates only the mediation-type leakage $B_{\mathrm{med}}(C)$, which has the same structure as in~§\ref{app:mediation}.
452	
453	\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}
454	At $C=I$: $B_{\mathrm{med}}(I)=0$ (controlling $A$ blocks the $T\to A\to Y$ path), and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(I)=\lambda\alpha_U/\operatorname{Var}(T)$ (pure confounding residual).
455	
456	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$: $B_{\mathrm{med}}=\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\operatorname{Var}(T)$, and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{nc}}=[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))+\lambda\alpha_U]/\operatorname{Var}(T)$, the naive regression coefficient with no controls.
457	
458	\paragraph{Empirical Finding (Front-door sign preservation).}
459	\label{find:fd_sign}
460	Since $B_{\mathrm{med}}(C)$ has the same structure as the mediation bias (§\ref{app:mediation})---interpolating between $0$ at $C{=}I$ and $\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\operatorname{Var}(T)$ at $C$ uniform---sign preservation holds whenever the two endpoints have the same sign:
461	\[
462	  \operatorname{sign}\!\bigl(\lambda\alpha_U\bigr)
463	  = \operatorname{sign}\!\bigl(\lambda\alpha_U + \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))\bigr).
464	\]
465	This result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$.
466	
467	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
468	With $\alpha_U{=}1.0$, $\lambda{=}1.0$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\boldsymbol{\gamma}{=}(0,0.5,{-}0.3)$, $\boldsymbol{\delta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.8)$:
469	\begin{align*}
470	  \lambda\alpha_U/\operatorname{Var}(T) &= 1.0\times 1.0\,/\,2.0 = 0.500,\\
471	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\operatorname{Var}(T) &= 0.594\,/\,2.0 = 0.297.
472	\end{align*}
473	Both components are positive, so $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C)\in[0.500,\,0.797]>0$ for all $C$.
474	
475	\subsection{Exposure Topology: Sign Reversal Counterexample}
476	\label{app:exposure}
477	
478	\begin{proposition}[Exposure sign reversal]
479	\label{prop:exp_counter}
480	For $K{=}3$ with $A$ as the treatment variable, there exist a marginal distribution $\mathbf{p}$, treatment effects $\boldsymbol{\beta}$, and a column-stochastic $C$ with $C_{kk}>0.5$ for all $k$, such that $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_1)\neq\operatorname{sign}(\beta_1)$.
481	\end{proposition}
482	
483	\begin{proof}[Construction]
484	Consider the exposure DGP $Y = \beta_1 D_1 + \beta_2 D_2 + \varepsilon_Y$ with:
485	\begin{itemize}
486	  \item Uniform prior: $\mathbf{p}=(1/3,\,1/3,\,1/3)$.
487	  \item True effects: $\beta_1=1$,\; $\beta_2=-10$.
488	\end{itemize}
489	Choose the confusion matrix
490	\[
491	  C = \begin{pmatrix}
492	    0.60 & 0.10 & 0.10\\
493	    0.10 & 0.60 & 0.39\\
494	    0.30 & 0.30 & 0.51
495	  \end{pmatrix},\qquad
496	  C_{kk}\in\{0.60,\,0.60,\,0.51\},\;\text{all}>0.5.
497	\]
498	Column sums are $1$ (verifiable by inspection).
499	
500	The OLS estimator regresses $Y$ on $(1,D_1^*,D_2^*)$.
501	The plim of $\hat\beta_j$ equals $\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}j]-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}0]$, where
502	\[
503	  \mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}j] = \sum_{k=0}^{2} P(A{=}k\mid A^*{=}j)\,\beta_k
504	  = \sum_{k=1}^{2}\frac{C_{jk}\,p_k}{q_j}\,\beta_k.
505	\]
506	
507	\emph{Marginal distribution of $A^*$:}
508	\begin{align*}
509	  q_0 &= \tfrac{1}{3}(0.60+0.10+0.10)=0.267,\\
510	  q_1 &= \tfrac{1}{3}(0.10+0.60+0.39)=0.363,\\
511	  q_2 &= \tfrac{1}{3}(0.30+0.30+0.51)=0.370.
512	\end{align*}
513	
514	\emph{Conditional means:}
515	\begin{align*}
516	  \mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}0]
517	  &= \frac{0.10\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times 1 + 0.10\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times({-}10)}{0.267}
518	  = \frac{0.0333-0.3333}{0.267} = -1.125,\\[4pt]
519	  \mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}1]
520	  &= \frac{0.60\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times 1 + 0.39\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times({-}10)}{0.363}
521	  = \frac{0.200-1.300}{0.363} = -3.028,\\[4pt]
522	  \mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}2]
523	  &= \frac{0.30\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times 1 + 0.51\times\tfrac{1}{3}\times({-}10)}{0.370}
524	  = \frac{0.100-1.700}{0.370} = -4.324.
525	\end{align*}
526	
527	\emph{Estimated coefficient:}
528	\[
529	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_1 = \mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}1]-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*{=}0]
530	  = -3.028-(-1.125) = \mathbf{-1.903}.
531	\]
532	The true $\beta_1=+1>0$ but $\operatorname{plim}\hat\beta_1=-1.903<0$: \textbf{sign reversal}.
533	
534	\emph{Mechanism.}
535	The large off-diagonal entry $C_{12}=0.39$ (probability of classifying true category~2 as $A^*{=}1$) routes substantial probability mass from category~2 ($\beta_2=-10$) into the $A^*{=}1$ bin.
536	Despite $C_{11}=0.60>0.5$, the contamination from the strongly negative $\beta_2$ overwhelms the positive $\beta_1$, reversing the sign of the estimated effect for category~1.
537	This failure mode is structurally impossible at $K{=}2$: a $2{\times}2$ column-stochastic matrix with $C_{kk}>0.5$ has the form $C=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}a&1{-}b\\1{-}a&b\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ with $a,b>0.5$, and the single coefficient $\hat\beta_1$ is a positive-weighted average of $\beta_1$, preserving sign.
538	For $K{\geq}3$, the additional degrees of freedom in $C$ permit cross-contamination between categories with opposite-sign effects.
539	\end{proof}
540	
541	\subsection{Summary of Conditions}
542	\label{app:conditions}
543	
544	\begin{table}[h]
545	\centering
546	\small
547	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
548	\toprule
549	\textbf{Topology} & \textbf{Sign preservation condition} & \textbf{Paper DGP} \\
550	\midrule
551	Confounding & $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{OV}})$ & $\checkmark$ ($2.0$ vs $2.3$)\\
552	Mediation & $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$ & $\checkmark$ ($0.5$ vs $0.85$)\\
553	Collider & $|\tau|>\bar{B}_{\mathrm{col}}$ (Prop.~\ref{prop:col_gen}) & $\checkmark$ ($1.0$ vs $0.15$)\\
554	M-bias & Bias is attenuating (toward $\tau$) & $\checkmark$ \\
555	Front-door & $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{OV}})$ (§\ref{app:frontdoor}) & $\checkmark$ ($0.50$ vs $0.80$)\\
556	\midrule
557	Exposure & Not guaranteed for $K\geq 3$ & Counterexample above \\
558	IV & Not guaranteed (denominator instability) & --- \\
559	\bottomrule
560	\end{tabular}
561	\caption{Sufficient conditions for sign preservation under non-differential misclassification.
562	For confounding, mediation, and front-door, the condition requires the true (or clean-control) effect and the no-control effect to have the same sign.
563	For the collider, the condition bounds the Berkson's bias magnitude relative to $|\tau|$.
564	All conditions are verified for our DGP parameters.}
565	\label{tab:sign_conditions}
566	\end{table}
567	
568	\paragraph{Structural argument for M-bias topology.}
569	\emph{M-bias:} The M-structure creates a backdoor path through two latent common causes.
570	Misclassification of $A$ weakens the statistical association along this backdoor path, reducing the spurious correlation rather than amplifying it.
571	The bias therefore interpolates between zero (perfect classification) and the full backdoor bias (no classification information), preserving sign under the same sufficient condition as confounding.
572	
573	\paragraph{Remark.}
574	The sign preservation conditions for confounding, mediation, collider, and front-door are conditions on the DGP parameters (the relative strength of the causal effect versus the confounding, indirect, or collider-induced bias), not on the confusion matrix $C$.
575	Once the condition is satisfied, sign preservation holds for \emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ with $C_{kk}>0$---no diagonal-dominance restriction is needed.
576	Conversely, if the condition fails (i.e., the omitted-variable or total-effect estimate has the opposite sign from $\tau$), then there exist confusion matrices that produce sign reversal.
577	For the DGP parameters used throughout our experiments, the conditions are comfortably satisfied.
578

Naive/MLA show 0.0\% for FWL-invariant topologies because $C^{-1}$ cannot change the $T$ coefficient. \textbf{Bold}: best method per topology; \underline{underline}: best gold-free method.}

619	\begin{algorithm}[h]
620	\caption{Correction method selection for LLM-annotated causal inference.}
621	\label{alg:correction}
622	\small
623	\begin{algorithmic}[1]
624	\REQUIRE DAG topology $\mathcal{T}$, gold-label availability $G \in \{\textsc{yes}, \textsc{no}\}$
625	\ENSURE Recommended method $M$, risk level $R$
626	\STATE
627	\STATE \textbf{Step 1: Assess risk level}
628	\IF{$\mathcal{T} \in \{\text{M-bias}\}$}
629	    \STATE $R \gets \textsc{Low}$ \COMMENT{$|\text{bias}| < 0.04$ for all $C$}
630	\ELSIF{$\mathcal{T} \in \{\text{Conf., Med., Coll., Front-door}\}$}
631	    \STATE $R \gets \textsc{Medium}$ \COMMENT{bounded bias, no sign reversal}
632	\ELSE
633	    \STATE $R \gets \textsc{High}$ \COMMENT{Exposure, IV: sign reversal 83.4\% / 52.6\%}
634	\ENDIF
635	\STATE
636	\STATE \textbf{Step 2: Select correction method}
637	\IF{$G = \textsc{yes}$}
638	    \IF{$\mathcal{T} = \text{M-bias}$}
639	        \STATE $M \gets \textsc{None}$ \COMMENT{PPI++ only 63.7\%; no correction preferred}
640	    \ELSE
641	        \STATE $M \gets \text{PPI++}$ \COMMENT{93.8\% mean bias red., 96.8\% coverage}
642	    \ENDIF
643	\ELSE
644	    \STATE \COMMENT{no gold labels}
645	    \IF{$\mathcal{T} \in \{\text{Confounding, Mediation}\}$}
646	        \STATE $M \gets \text{DSL}$ \COMMENT{49.8\% / 70.3\% bias reduction}
647	    \ELSIF{$\mathcal{T} = \text{IV}$}
648	        \STATE $M \gets \text{MC-SIMEX}$ \COMMENT{64.7\% bias reduction}
649	    \ELSIF{$\mathcal{T} \in \{\text{Collider, M-bias}\}$}
650	        \STATE $M \gets \text{MC-SIMEX}$ \COMMENT{37.1\% / 25.7\% bias reduction}
651	    \ELSE
652	        \STATE $M \gets \emptyset$; flag: acquire gold labels \COMMENT{no reliable gold-free method}
653	    \ENDIF
654	\ENDIF
655	\STATE
656	\STATE \textbf{Step 3: FWL invariance warning}
657	\IF{$\mathcal{T} \in \{\text{Conf., Med., Coll., M-bias}\}$}
658	    \STATE Skip Naive plug-in and MLA \COMMENT{0\% bias red.\ on $T$ coeff.}
659	\ENDIF
660	\RETURN $(M, R)$
661	\end{algorithmic}
662	\end{algorithm}
```

```json
      "content": "\\section{Proof of Proposition~\\ref{prop:signflip}}\n\\label{app:proof}\n\nWe provide algebraic proofs of sign preservation for the confounding, collider, mediation, and front-door topologies, and an explicit counterexample for the exposure topology.\nThroughout, $A\\in\\{0,1,\\dots,K{-}1\\}$ with prior $p_k=P(A{=}k)$, and $C$ is a $K{\\times}K$ column-stochastic confusion matrix with $C_{jk}=P(A^*{=}j\\mid A{=}k)$.\nWe use category~0 as the reference and let $D_j=\\mathbf{1}(A{=}j)$, $D_j^*=\\mathbf{1}(A^*{=}j)$ for $j=1,\\dots,K{-}1$.\n\n\\subsection{Notation and Key Identities}\n\\label{app:notation}\n\nUnder non-differential misclassification, the misclassified moments factor linearly through $C$.\nDefine the population vectors:\n\\begin{align}\n  q_j &= P(A^*{=}j) = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,p_k, \\label{eq:q}\\\\\n  \\mathrm{ct}_j &= \\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D_j^*] = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,\\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D_k], \\label{eq:ct}\\\\\n  \\mathrm{cy}_j &= \\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_j^*] = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_k]. \\label{eq:cy}\n\\end{align}\nWrite $q = Cp$, $\\mathrm{ct} = C\\,\\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D]$, and $\\mathrm{cy} = C\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D]$, where $D=(D_0,\\dots,D_{K-1})^\\top$.\n\n\\subsection{Frisch--Waugh--Lovell Decomposition}\n\\label{app:fwl}\n\nFor topologies where $T$ is directly observed (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door), the estimator regresses $Y$ on $Z^*=(1,T,D_1^*,\\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.\nBy the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell (FWL) theorem, the coefficient on $T$ satisfies:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat{\\tau}(C)\n  = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,\\tilde{Y}]}{\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]},\n  \\label{eq:fwl}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\tilde{T}=T-\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]$ and $\\tilde{Y}=Y-\\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*]$ are residuals from projecting onto $(1,D_1^*,\\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.\n\n\\begin{lemma}[Bias decomposition]\n\\label{lem:bias_decomp}\nSuppose $Y = \\tau T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y$ with $\\varepsilon_Y \\perp (T, A, A^*)$.\nThen\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\tau + \\frac{R(C)}{D(C)},\n  \\label{eq:bias_decomp}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere\n\\begin{align}\n  R(C) &= \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)\\bigr], \\label{eq:R}\\\\\n  D(C) &= \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)\\bigr] > 0. \\label{eq:D}\n\\end{align}\n\\end{lemma}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nExpand $\\tilde{Y}$ using $Y=\\tau T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y$:\n\\[\n  \\tilde{Y} = \\tau\\,\\tilde{T} + \\bigl(f(A) - \\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr) + \\varepsilon_Y.\n\\]\nSince $\\tilde{T}$ is measurable w.r.t.\\ $(T,A^*)$ and $\\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A,A^*)$:\n\\[\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,\\tilde{Y}]\n  = \\tau\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]\n  + \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\tilde{T}\\,\\bigl(f(A)-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr)\\bigr].\n\\]\nThe numerator residual is $R(C) = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}(f(A)-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*])]$, and the denominator is $D(C) = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]$.\n\nBy the law of total covariance:\n\\[\n  R(C) = \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1} q_j\\,\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  = \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)].\n\\]\nFor $D(C)>0$: decompose $T = g(A) + \\varepsilon_T$ where $g(A)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A]$ (for confounding) or simply note that $T$ has exogenous variation independent of $A^*$ (for mediation).\nIn both cases, $\\tilde{T}=\\bigl(g(A)-\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr)+\\varepsilon_T'$, where $\\varepsilon_T'$ is the component of $T$ independent of $(A,A^*)$.\nHence $D(C)\\geq\\operatorname{Var}(\\varepsilon_T')>0$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\subsection{Confounding Topology ($A$ = Confounder)}\n\\label{app:confounding}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$A\\to T$, $A\\to Y$, $T\\to Y$.\nUsing dummy coding with category 0 as reference:\n\\begin{align*}\n  T &= \\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\\alpha_j D_j + \\varepsilon_T,\\quad\n  \\varepsilon_T\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,\\sigma_T^2),\\;\\varepsilon_T\\perp A,\\\\\n  Y &= \\tau\\, T + \\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\\beta_j^{(A)} D_j + \\varepsilon_Y,\\quad\n  \\varepsilon_Y\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\;\\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A).\n\\end{align*}\nDefine $g(k)=\\sum_{j}\\alpha_j\\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$ and $f(k)=\\sum_{j}\\beta_j^{(A)}\\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$, so $g(0)=f(0)=0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Closed-form plim.}\nUsing the FWL decomposition, the coefficient on $T$ is:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C)\n  = \\frac{\\mu_{TY} - \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\\frac{\\mathrm{ct}_j\\,\\mathrm{cy}_j}{q_j}}\n  {s_T - \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\\frac{\\mathrm{ct}_j^2}{q_j}},\n  \\label{eq:plim_conf}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $s_T=\\mathbb{E}[T^2]$, $\\mu_{TY}=\\mathbb{E}[TY]$, and $\\mathrm{ct}_j$, $\\mathrm{cy}_j$, $q_j$ are defined in~\\eqref{eq:q}--\\eqref{eq:cy}.\n\n\\begin{proof}[Derivation]\nBy FWL, $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}Y]/\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]$ where $\\tilde{T}=T-\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]$.\nSince $\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*{=}j]=\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]\n  &= \\mathbb{E}[T^2] - \\sum_j q_j\\bigl(\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\\bigr)^2\n  = s_T - \\sum_j \\mathrm{ct}_j^2/q_j,\\\\\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,Y]\n  &= \\mathbb{E}[TY] - \\sum_j \\bigl(\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\\bigr)\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_j^*]\n  = \\mu_{TY} - \\sum_j \\mathrm{ct}_j\\,\\mathrm{cy}_j/q_j.\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Verification at $C=I$.}\nWhen $C=I$: $q_j=p_j$, $\\mathrm{ct}_j=\\mathbb{E}[T D_j]=\\alpha_j p_j$, $\\mathrm{cy}_j=\\mathbb{E}[Y D_j]=p_j(\\tau\\alpha_j+\\beta_j^{(A)})$.\nDirect substitution yields numerator $= \\tau\\sigma_T^2$ and denominator $= \\sigma_T^2$, giving $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\tau$.\n\n\\paragraph{Bias structure.}\nBy Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\tau + B(C),\\quad\n  B(C) = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.\n  \\label{eq:bias_conf}\n\\end{equation}\nBy the law of total covariance:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\\mid A^*)]\n  = \\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))\n  - \\operatorname{Cov}\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*],\\;\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr).\n  \\label{eq:lotc}\n\\end{equation}\n\nAt $C=I$: $\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*]=g(A)$, so the between-group covariance equals $\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ and $B(I)=0$.\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ (uniform, $A^*\\perp A$): $\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*]$ is constant, so the between-group covariance is zero and\n\\begin{equation}\n  B_{\\mathrm{OV}} \\;\\equiv\\; B\\bigl(\\tfrac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top\\bigr)\n  = \\frac{\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\\,f(A))}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)},\n  \\label{eq:bov}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich equals the classical omitted-variable bias (no control for $A$).\n\n\\paragraph{Sign preservation condition.}\nSince $D(C)>0$, sign$(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau) = $ sign$(\\tau\\cdot D(C) + R(C))$.\nFor sign preservation, it suffices that $R(C)$ does not overwhelm $\\tau\\cdot D(C)$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- proportional case]\n\\label{prop:conf_prop}\nIf $f(A) = \\lambda\\,g(A)$ for some constant $\\lambda$ (i.e., the confounder effects on $T$ and $Y$ are proportional), then\n\\begin{equation}\n  B(C) = \\bigl(1-\\eta(C)\\bigr)\\,B_{\\mathrm{OV}},\n  \\label{eq:convex}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere\n\\[\n  \\eta(C) = \\frac{\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])\\;\\sigma_T^2}\n  {\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\bigl(\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])+\\sigma_T^2\\bigr)}\n  \\;\\in\\;[0,1].\n\\]\nConsequently, $B(C)$ lies between $0$ and $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$, and $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ whenever $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau+B_{\\mathrm{OV}})$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nWhen $f=\\lambda g$: the between-group covariance in~\\eqref{eq:lotc} becomes $\\lambda\\,\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])$, and $\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)=\\lambda\\,\\operatorname{Var}(g)$.\nSo $R(C)=\\lambda\\bigl(\\operatorname{Var}(g)-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])\\bigr)=\\lambda\\,V_W$, where $V_W=\\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ with $V_B=\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])$.\nSimilarly, $D(C)=V_W+\\sigma_T^2$.\nHence\n\\[\n  B(C) = \\frac{\\lambda\\,V_W}{V_W+\\sigma_T^2},\\qquad\n  B_{\\mathrm{OV}} = \\frac{\\lambda\\,V}{V+\\sigma_T^2},\n\\]\nwhere $V=\\operatorname{Var}(g)=V_B+V_W$.\nThe ratio\n\\[\n  \\frac{B(C)}{B_{\\mathrm{OV}}} = \\frac{V_W(V+\\sigma_T^2)}{V(V_W+\\sigma_T^2)} = 1 - \\eta(C)\n\\]\nis a decreasing function of $V_B\\in[0,V]$.\nAt $V_B=0$ (no classification, $C$ uniform): $\\eta=0$, $B=B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$.\nAt $V_B=V$ (perfect classification, $C=I$): $\\eta=1$, $B=0$.\nSince $0\\leq V_B\\leq V$, we have $\\eta\\in[0,1]$ and $B(C)\\in[0,B_{\\mathrm{OV}}]$ (with the sign of $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$).\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- general case]\n\\label{prop:conf_gen}\nFor general $g$ and $f$ (not necessarily proportional), define the worst-case bias bound\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\bar{B} \\;=\\; \\frac{\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f(A))}}{\\sigma_T^2}.\n  \\label{eq:bbar}\n\\end{equation}\nIf $|\\tau| > \\bar{B}$, then $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ for \\emph{all} column-stochastic $C$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nWe need $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau\\cdot D(C)+R(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$, which holds if $|R(C)|<|\\tau|\\cdot D(C)$.\nSince $D(C)\\geq\\sigma_T^2$ (shown in Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}), it suffices to show $|R(C)|<|\\tau|\\,\\sigma_T^2$.\n\n\\emph{Step 1: Bound $|R(C)|$.}\n$R(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A)\\mid A^*)]$.\nBy the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to each within-group covariance:\n\\[\n  |\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\\mid A^*{=}j)|\n  \\leq \\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*{=}j)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*{=}j)}.\n\\]\nTaking expectations and applying Cauchy--Schwarz again:\n\\begin{align*}\n  |R(C)|\n  &\\leq \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*)}\\bigr]\\\\\n  &\\leq \\sqrt{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*)]\\;\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*)]}\\\\\n  &\\leq \\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\;\\operatorname{Var}(f(A))},\n\\end{align*}\nwhere the last step uses $\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(X\\mid Y)]\\leq\\operatorname{Var}(X)$ (law of total variance).\n\n\\emph{Step 2: Conclude.}\nThe bias satisfies\n\\[\n  |B(C)| = \\frac{|R(C)|}{D(C)} \\leq \\frac{\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f)}}{\\sigma_T^2} = \\bar{B}.\n\\]\nWhen $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}$, the bias cannot cancel $\\tau$, so $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ for all $C$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to the omitted-variable condition.}\nA weaker (necessary) condition for sign preservation is $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}})$ where $\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}}=\\tau+\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$.\nIf this fails, then $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ (uniform noise) already flips the sign.\nThe rigorous sufficient condition $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}$ implies the weaker condition, since $|B_{\\mathrm{OV}}|=|\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)|/\\operatorname{Var}(T)\\leq\\bar{B}$ by Cauchy--Schwarz and $\\operatorname{Var}(T)\\geq\\sigma_T^2$.\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\tau{=}2.0$, $\\boldsymbol{\\alpha}{=}(1.5,{-}1.0)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}^{(A)}{=}(1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\\sigma_T{=}1.0$, $\\mathbf{p}{=}(0.40,0.35,0.25)$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\operatorname{Var}(g) &= \\sum_k p_k\\alpha_k^2 - \\bigl(\\textstyle\\sum_k p_k\\alpha_k\\bigr)^2 = 0.962,\\\\\n  \\operatorname{Var}(f) &= \\sum_k p_k(\\beta_k^{(A)})^2 - \\bigl(\\textstyle\\sum_k p_k\\beta_k^{(A)}\\bigr)^2 = 0.362,\\\\\n  \\bar{B} &= \\sqrt{0.962\\times 0.362}\\;/\\;1.0^2 = 0.590.\n\\end{align*}\nSince $|\\tau|{=}2.0 > \\bar{B}{=}0.590$, sign preservation is guaranteed for \\emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ by Proposition~\\ref{prop:conf_gen}.\nAdditionally, $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}=\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.588/1.962={+}0.300$, giving $\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}}{=}2.300>0$, confirming that both the necessary and sufficient conditions are satisfied.\n\n\\subsection{Mediation Topology ($A$ = Mediator)}\n\\label{app:mediation}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$T\\to A\\to Y$, $T\\to Y$.\n\\begin{align*}\n  T &\\sim \\operatorname{Bernoulli}(0.5),\\\\\n  A\\mid T &\\sim \\operatorname{Categorical}\\bigl(\\operatorname{softmax}(\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}+\\boldsymbol{\\delta}\\,T)\\bigr),\\\\\n  Y &= \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}\\,T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y.\n\\end{align*}\n\n\\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}\nBy Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}, the direct-effect estimator satisfies:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C) = \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}} + \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.\n  \\label{eq:med_bias}\n\\end{equation}\n\nThe key structural difference from confounding: $T$ \\emph{causes} $A$, so the residual covariance $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)$ captures the indirect effect of $T$ on $Y$ through $A$ that is not absorbed by conditioning on $A^*$.\n\n\\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}\n\nAt $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$): conditioning on $A$ blocks the mediated path $T\\to A\\to Y$, so $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A)=0$ and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau=\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$.\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ ($A^*\\perp A$): no information about $A$ is used, and\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)] &= \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)),\\\\\n  D(C) &= \\operatorname{Var}(T) = 0.25.\n\\end{align*}\nThe indirect effect is $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0]$, and\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)) = 0.25\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}.\n\\]\nHence at $C$ uniform: $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}} = \\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Finding (Mediation sign preservation).}\n\\label{find:med_sign}\n$\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})$ whenever $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$, where $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}=\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\nThis result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$; a closed-form proof for general $K$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{proof}\nLet $R(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)]$ and $D(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]>0$.\n\n\\emph{Endpoints.}\nAt $C{=}I$: $f(A)$ is constant given $A{=}A^*$, so $R(I)=0$ and $B(I)=0$.\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$: $R=\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))=0.25\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $D=\\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.25$, giving $B=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\n\n\\emph{Within-group structure for binary $T$.}\nSince $T\\in\\{0,1\\}$, the within-$A^*$-group bias admits a revealing decomposition.\nLet $p_j=P(T{=}1\\mid A^*{=}j)$.\nThen $\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*{=}j)=p_j(1{-}p_j)$ and\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  = p_j(1{-}p_j)\\bigl[\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]\\bigr].\n\\]\nDefine $\\delta_j=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]$ as the \\emph{conditional indirect effect} in the $A^*{=}j$ subpopulation.\nThen\n\\[\n  B(C)=\\frac{R(C)}{D(C)}\n  =\\frac{\\sum_j q_j\\,p_j(1{-}p_j)\\,\\delta_j}{\\sum_j q_j\\,p_j(1{-}p_j)}\n  =\\sum_j w_j\\,\\delta_j,\n\\]\na weighted average of the conditional indirect effects, with weights $w_j=q_j p_j(1{-}p_j)/\\sum_\\ell q_\\ell p_\\ell(1{-}p_\\ell)\\geq 0$ summing to 1.\n\nAt $C{=}I$: each $A^*{=}j$ group has $A{=}j$ a.s., so $\\delta_j=f(j)-f(j)=0$ and $B=0$.\nAt $C$ uniform: $\\delta_j=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ for all $j$ and $B=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\n\n\\emph{Sign preservation.}\nWhen $\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))>0$ (the indirect effect reinforces the direct effect, i.e., $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ have the same sign), the bias $B(C)\\geq 0$ cannot oppose $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$, so sign is preserved trivially.\n\nWhen $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ have opposite signs but $|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|<|\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}|$ (i.e., $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$), sign reversal requires some $|\\delta_j|>|\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}|$ with the opposing sign.\nSince each $\\delta_j$ is a difference of weighted averages of $f$ values (with weights $P(A{=}k\\mid T{=}t,A^*{=}j)$), and $B(C)$ is a convex combination of $\\delta_j$, the bound $|B(C)|\\leq\\max_j|\\delta_j|$ holds.\nWe verify numerically that $\\max_j|\\delta_j|\\leq|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|$ for all 100{,}000 random $C$ matrices in our experiments, confirming sign preservation.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}{=}0.5$, $\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}{=}(0,0,0)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\delta}{=}(0,0.8,{-}0.6)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$:\n\n$\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0]=0.351$ (computed from the softmax class probabilities).\n$\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}=0.5+0.351=0.851>0$.\nSince $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}{>}0$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}{>}0$, sign preservation holds for all $C$.\n\n\\subsection{Collider Topology ($A$ = Collider)}\n\\label{app:collider}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$T\\to A\\leftarrow Y$, $T\\to Y$.\nThe treatment effect is unconfounded:\n\\begin{align*}\n  T &\\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\quad\n  Y = \\tau\\,T + \\varepsilon_Y,\\quad \\varepsilon_Y\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\;\\varepsilon_Y\\perp T,\\\\\n  A\\mid T,Y &\\sim \\operatorname{Categorical}\\bigl(\\operatorname{softmax}(\\boldsymbol{\\delta}_T\\,T + \\boldsymbol{\\delta}_Y\\,Y)\\bigr).\n\\end{align*}\nThe collider $A$ is a common \\emph{effect} of $T$ and $Y$.\nThe no-control estimator (regressing $Y$ on $T$ alone) is consistent: $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{nc}}=\\tau$.\nThe researcher erroneously controls for $A^*$, inducing Berkson's bias.\n\n\\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}\nUnlike confounding, $\\varepsilon_Y$ is not independent of $A$ (since $A$ depends on $Y=\\tau T+\\varepsilon_Y$), so Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp} does not apply directly.\nInstead, we derive the bias from the FWL identity~\\eqref{eq:fwl}.\nWriting $\\tilde{Y}=\\tau\\tilde{T}+(\\varepsilon_Y-\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_Y\\mid A^*])$ and noting that $\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,\\varepsilon_Y]=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\varepsilon_Y\\mid A^*)]$ (since $T\\perp\\varepsilon_Y$ unconditionally):\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\tau + B(C),\\quad\n  B(C) = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,\\varepsilon_Y\\mid A^*)]}{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]}.\n  \\label{eq:bias_col}\n\\end{equation}\nUnder non-differential misclassification ($A^*\\perp(T,\\varepsilon_Y)\\mid A$), define $g(k)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A{=}k]$ and $h(k)=\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_Y\\mid A{=}k]$.\nBy the tower property, $\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]=\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*]$ and $\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_Y\\mid A^*]=\\mathbb{E}[h(A)\\mid A^*]$.\nSince $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\varepsilon_Y)=0$, the law of total covariance gives:\n\\[\n  B(C) = \\frac{-\\operatorname{Cov}\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*],\\;\\mathbb{E}[h(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr)}{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]}.\n\\]\n\n\\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}\nAt $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$):\n$B(I) = -\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),h(A))\\,/\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)] = B_{\\mathrm{Berkson}}$,\nthe full Berkson's bias from conditioning on the collider.\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ ($A^*\\perp A$):\n$\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*]$ and $\\mathbb{E}[h(A)\\mid A^*]$ are constant, so $B=0$ and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau=\\tau$.\n\nThis is the structural reverse of confounding: misclassification attenuates the collider-induced bias toward zero, rather than growing bias from zero toward $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Collider sign preservation --- proportional case]\n\\label{prop:col_prop}\nIf $h(A)=\\lambda\\,g(A)$ for some constant $\\lambda$, then $|B(C)|\\leq|B(I)|$ for all column-stochastic $C$, and $B(C)$ has the same sign as $B(I)$.\nConsequently, $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ whenever $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau+B_{\\mathrm{Berkson}})$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nWhen $h=\\lambda g$, the numerator becomes $-\\lambda\\,\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])$ and the denominator is $\\operatorname{Var}(T)-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])$.\nLet $V^*=\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])\\in[0,V_g]$ where $V_g=\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$.\nThen\n\\[\n  |B(C)| = |\\lambda|\\,\\frac{V^*}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)-V^*}.\n\\]\nThe function $x\\mapsto x/(a-x)$ is increasing on $[0,a)$.\nWith $a=\\operatorname{Var}(T)>V_g\\geq V^*$:\n$|B(C)|\\leq|\\lambda|\\,V_g/(\\operatorname{Var}(T)-V_g)=|B(I)|$.\nAt $V^*{=}0$ (uniform $C$): $B{=}0$.  At $V^*{=}V_g$ ($C{=}I$): $B{=}B(I)$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Collider sign preservation --- general case]\n\\label{prop:col_gen}\nDefine\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}} = \\frac{\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\,\\operatorname{Var}(h(A))}}{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)]}.\n  \\label{eq:bbar_col}\n\\end{equation}\nIf $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}}$, then $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ for all column-stochastic $C$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\n\\emph{Step~1: Bound the numerator.}\nBy Cauchy--Schwarz:\n$|\\operatorname{Cov}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*],\\mathbb{E}[h\\mid A^*])|\\leq\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])\\,\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[h\\mid A^*])}$.\nSince conditional expectation reduces variance: $\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])\\leq\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$ and likewise for $h$.\n\n\\emph{Step~2: Bound the denominator.}\n$\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]=\\operatorname{Var}(T)-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*])\\geq\\operatorname{Var}(T)-\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)]$.\n\n\\emph{Step~3: Conclude.}\n$|B(C)|\\leq\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(h)}\\,/\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)]=\\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}}$.\nWhen $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}}$, the bias cannot overwhelm $\\tau$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\tau{=}1.0$, $\\boldsymbol{\\delta}_T{=}(0,\\,0.5,\\,{-}0.3)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\delta}_Y{=}(0,\\,0.8,\\,0.6)$, from $5{\\times}10^6$-sample Monte Carlo:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\operatorname{Var}(g(A)) &= 0.187,\\quad\n  \\operatorname{Var}(h(A)) = 0.076,\\quad\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)] = 0.813,\\\\\n  \\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}} &= \\sqrt{0.187\\times 0.076}\\;/\\;0.813 = 0.147.\n\\end{align*}\nSince $|\\tau|{=}1.0\\gg\\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}}{=}0.147$, Proposition~\\ref{prop:col_gen} guarantees sign preservation.\nAdditionally, $B_{\\mathrm{Berkson}}{=}{-}0.114$, giving $\\tau+B_{\\mathrm{Berkson}}{=}0.886{>}0$.\nVerified across $100{,}000$ random column-stochastic $C$: $\\tau_{\\mathrm{sign\\,flip}}{=}0\\%$, and $|B(C)|\\leq|B_{\\mathrm{Berkson}}|$ in all cases.\n\n\\subsection{Front-door Topology ($A$ = Mediator under Confounding)}\n\\label{app:frontdoor}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$T\\to A\\to Y$, $U\\to T$, $U\\to Y$.\nAn unobserved confounder $U$ affects both treatment and outcome:\n\\begin{align*}\n  U &\\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\quad\n  T = \\alpha_U\\,U + \\eta,\\quad \\eta\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\;\\eta\\perp U,\\\\\n  A\\mid T &\\sim \\operatorname{Categorical}\\bigl(\\operatorname{softmax}(\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}+\\boldsymbol{\\delta}\\,T)\\bigr),\\\\\n  Y &= f(A) + \\lambda\\,U + \\varepsilon_Y,\\quad \\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A,U).\n\\end{align*}\nThere is no direct $T{\\to}Y$ edge; the causal effect of $T$ on $Y$ operates entirely through $A$.\nThe researcher controls for $A^*$ in $Y\\sim 1+T+D_1^*+\\cdots+D_{K-1}^*$.\n\n\\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}\nSince the direct effect is zero, the FWL coefficient on $T$ captures only bias:\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,Y\\mid A^*)]}{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]}.\n\\]\nSubstituting $Y=f(A)+\\lambda U+\\varepsilon_Y$ and using $\\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A,A^*)$:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C)\n  = \\underbrace{\\frac{\\lambda\\,\\alpha_U}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)}}_{\\text{confounding (constant)}}\n  \\;+\\; \\underbrace{\\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}}_{\\displaystyle B_{\\mathrm{med}}(C)},\n  \\label{eq:fd_decomp}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $D(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]$.\n\n\\begin{proof}[Derivation of~\\eqref{eq:fd_decomp}]\nExpand the numerator as\n$\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)] + \\lambda\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,U\\mid A^*)]$.\nFor the confounding term: since $T=\\alpha_U U+\\eta$ with $(U,\\eta)$ jointly Gaussian, the regression residual $\\xi=U-\\frac{\\alpha_U}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)}T$ satisfies $\\xi\\perp T$.\nBecause $A$ is a function of $T$ (plus independent softmax noise) and $A^*$ is a function of $A$ (plus independent misclassification noise), $\\xi\\perp(T,A,A^*)$.\nTherefore\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,U\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  = \\frac{\\alpha_U}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)}\\,\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  \\quad\\forall\\;j,\n\\]\nand $\\lambda\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,U\\mid A^*)]=\\frac{\\lambda\\alpha_U}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)}\\,D(C)$, yielding the constant first term after division by $D(C)$.\n\\end{proof}\n\nThis decomposition reveals that \\emph{misclassification does not affect the confounding component}.\nThe confusion matrix $C$ modulates only the mediation-type leakage $B_{\\mathrm{med}}(C)$, which has the same structure as in~§\\ref{app:mediation}.\n\n\\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}\nAt $C=I$: $B_{\\mathrm{med}}(I)=0$ (controlling $A$ blocks the $T\\to A\\to Y$ path), and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(I)=\\lambda\\alpha_U/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$ (pure confounding residual).\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$: $B_{\\mathrm{med}}=\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$, and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{nc}}=[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))+\\lambda\\alpha_U]/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$, the naive regression coefficient with no controls.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical Finding (Front-door sign preservation).}\n\\label{find:fd_sign}\nSince $B_{\\mathrm{med}}(C)$ has the same structure as the mediation bias (§\\ref{app:mediation})---interpolating between $0$ at $C{=}I$ and $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$ at $C$ uniform---sign preservation holds whenever the two endpoints have the same sign:\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{sign}\\!\\bigl(\\lambda\\alpha_U\\bigr)\n  = \\operatorname{sign}\\!\\bigl(\\lambda\\alpha_U + \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))\\bigr).\n\\]\nThis result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$.\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\alpha_U{=}1.0$, $\\lambda{=}1.0$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}{=}(0,0.5,{-}0.3)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\delta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.8)$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\lambda\\alpha_U/\\operatorname{Var}(T) &= 1.0\\times 1.0\\,/\\,2.0 = 0.500,\\\\\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))/\\operatorname{Var}(T) &= 0.594\\,/\\,2.0 = 0.297.\n\\end{align*}\nBoth components are positive, so $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C)\\in[0.500,\\,0.797]>0$ for all $C$.\n\n\\subsection{Exposure Topology: Sign Reversal Counterexample}\n\\label{app:exposure}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Exposure sign reversal]\n\\label{prop:exp_counter}\nFor $K{=}3$ with $A$ as the treatment variable, there exist a marginal distribution $\\mathbf{p}$, treatment effects $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$, and a column-stochastic $C$ with $C_{kk}>0.5$ for all $k$, such that $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\beta_1)\\neq\\operatorname{sign}(\\beta_1)$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}[Construction]\nConsider the exposure DGP $Y = \\beta_1 D_1 + \\beta_2 D_2 + \\varepsilon_Y$ with:\n\\begin{itemize}\n  \\item Uniform prior: $\\mathbf{p}=(1/3,\\,1/3,\\,1/3)$.\n  \\item True effects: $\\beta_1=1$,\\; $\\beta_2=-10$.\n\\end{itemize}\nChoose the confusion matrix\n\\[\n  C = \\begin{pmatrix}\n    0.60 & 0.10 & 0.10\\\\\n    0.10 & 0.60 & 0.39\\\\\n    0.30 & 0.30 & 0.51\n  \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n  C_{kk}\\in\\{0.60,\\,0.60,\\,0.51\\},\\;\\text{all}>0.5.\n\\]\nColumn sums are $1$ (verifiable by inspection).\n\nThe OLS estimator regresses $Y$ on $(1,D_1^*,D_2^*)$.\nThe plim of $\\hat\\beta_j$ equals $\\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}j]-\\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}0]$, where\n\\[\n  \\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}j] = \\sum_{k=0}^{2} P(A{=}k\\mid A^*{=}j)\\,\\beta_k\n  = \\sum_{k=1}^{2}\\frac{C_{jk}\\,p_k}{q_j}\\,\\beta_k.\n\\]\n\n\\emph{Marginal distribution of $A^*$:}\n\\begin{align*}\n  q_0 &= \\tfrac{1}{3}(0.60+0.10+0.10)=0.267,\\\\\n  q_1 &= \\tfrac{1}{3}(0.10+0.60+0.39)=0.363,\\\\\n  q_2 &= \\tfrac{1}{3}(0.30+0.30+0.51)=0.370.\n\\end{align*}\n\n\\emph{Conditional means:}\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}0]\n  &= \\frac{0.10\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times 1 + 0.10\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times({-}10)}{0.267}\n  = \\frac{0.0333-0.3333}{0.267} = -1.125,\\\\[4pt]\n  \\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}1]\n  &= \\frac{0.60\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times 1 + 0.39\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times({-}10)}{0.363}\n  = \\frac{0.200-1.300}{0.363} = -3.028,\\\\[4pt]\n  \\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}2]\n  &= \\frac{0.30\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times 1 + 0.51\\times\\tfrac{1}{3}\\times({-}10)}{0.370}\n  = \\frac{0.100-1.700}{0.370} = -4.324.\n\\end{align*}\n\n\\emph{Estimated coefficient:}\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\beta_1 = \\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}1]-\\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*{=}0]\n  = -3.028-(-1.125) = \\mathbf{-1.903}.\n\\]\nThe true $\\beta_1=+1>0$ but $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\beta_1=-1.903<0$: \\textbf{sign reversal}.\n\n\\emph{Mechanism.}\nThe large off-diagonal entry $C_{12}=0.39$ (probability of classifying true category~2 as $A^*{=}1$) routes substantial probability mass from category~2 ($\\beta_2=-10$) into the $A^*{=}1$ bin.\nDespite $C_{11}=0.60>0.5$, the contamination from the strongly negative $\\beta_2$ overwhelms the positive $\\beta_1$, reversing the sign of the estimated effect for category~1.\nThis failure mode is structurally impossible at $K{=}2$: a $2{\\times}2$ column-stochastic matrix with $C_{kk}>0.5$ has the form $C=\\bigl(\\begin{smallmatrix}a&1{-}b\\\\1{-}a&b\\end{smallmatrix}\\bigr)$ with $a,b>0.5$, and the single coefficient $\\hat\\beta_1$ is a positive-weighted average of $\\beta_1$, preserving sign.\nFor $K{\\geq}3$, the additional degrees of freedom in $C$ permit cross-contamination between categories with opposite-sign effects.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\subsection{Summary of Conditions}\n\\label{app:conditions}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Topology} & \\textbf{Sign preservation condition} & \\textbf{Paper DGP} \\\\\n\\midrule\nConfounding & $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}})$ & $\\checkmark$ ($2.0$ vs $2.3$)\\\\\nMediation & $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$ & $\\checkmark$ ($0.5$ vs $0.85$)\\\\\nCollider & $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}_{\\mathrm{col}}$ (Prop.~\\ref{prop:col_gen}) & $\\checkmark$ ($1.0$ vs $0.15$)\\\\\nM-bias & Bias is attenuating (toward $\\tau$) & $\\checkmark$ \\\\\nFront-door & $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}})$ (§\\ref{app:frontdoor}) & $\\checkmark$ ($0.50$ vs $0.80$)\\\\\n\\midrule\nExposure & Not guaranteed for $K\\geq 3$ & Counterexample above \\\\\nIV & Not guaranteed (denominator instability) & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Sufficient conditions for sign preservation under non-differential misclassification.\nFor confounding, mediation, and front-door, the condition requires the true (or clean-control) effect and the no-control effect to have the same sign.\nFor the collider, the condition bounds the Berkson's bias magnitude relative to $|\\tau|$.\nAll conditions are verified for our DGP parameters.}\n\\label{tab:sign_conditions}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Structural argument for M-bias topology.}\n\\emph{M-bias:} The M-structure creates a backdoor path through two latent common causes.\nMisclassification of $A$ weakens the statistical association along this backdoor path, reducing the spurious correlation rather than amplifying it.\nThe bias therefore interpolates between zero (perfect classification) and the full backdoor bias (no classification information), preserving sign under the same sufficient condition as confounding.\n\n\\paragraph{Remark.}\nThe sign preservation conditions for confounding, mediation, collider, and front-door are conditions on the DGP parameters (the relative strength of the causal effect versus the confounding, indirect, or collider-induced bias), not on the confusion matrix $C$.\nOnce the condition is satisfied, sign preservation holds for \\emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ with $C_{kk}>0$---no diagonal-dominance restriction is needed.\nConversely, if the condition fails (i.e., the omitted-variable or total-effect estimate has the opposite sign from $\\tau$), then there exist confusion matrices that produce sign reversal.\nFor the DGP parameters used throughout our experiments, the conditions are comfortably satisfied.\n\n\\section{Supplementary Tables, Figures, and Algorithm}\n\\label{app:supplementary}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig4_binarization.pdf}\n\\caption{Binarization collapse $\\Delta$ across topologies. Left: magnitude ASV ($\\delta^*$) range across 8 LLM configurations; lower values indicate greater fragility. ``Safe'' topologies never exceed the 10\\% bias threshold. method.}\n\\label{tab:correction}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Topology} & \\textbf{Naive} & \\textbf{MLA} & \\textbf{DSL} & \\textbf{SIMEX} & \\textbf{PPI++} +The collider $A$ is a common \emph{effect} of $T$ and $Y$.
```
```text
+The no-control estimator (regressing $Y$ on $T$ alone) is consistent: $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{nc}}=\tau$.

+\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}
+Unlike confounding, $\varepsilon_Y$ is not independent of $A$ (since $A$ depends on $Y=\tau T+\varepsilon_Y$), so Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp} does not apply directly.
+Instead, we derive the bias from the FWL identity~\eqref{eq:fwl}.
+Writing $\tilde{Y}=\tau\tilde{T}+(\varepsilon_Y-\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*])$ and noting that $\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\varepsilon_Y]=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*)]$ (since $T\perp\varepsilon_Y$ unconditionally):
+\begin{equation}
+  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + B(C),\quad
+  B(C) = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*)]}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]}.
+  \label{eq:bias_col}
+\end{equation}
+Under non-differential misclassification ($A^*\perp(T,\varepsilon_Y)\mid A$), define $g(k)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A{=}k]$ and $h(k)=\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A{=}k]$.
+By the tower property, $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]=\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]$ and $\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_Y\mid A^*]=\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]$.
+Since $\operatorname{Cov}(T,\varepsilon_Y)=0$, the law of total covariance gives:
+\[
+  B(C) = \frac{-\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*],\;\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)}{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]}.
+\]
+
+\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}
+At $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$):

+the full Berkson's bias from conditioning on the collider.

+At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ ($A^*\perp A$):
+$\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]$ and $\mathbb{E}[h(A)\mid A^*]$ are constant, so $B=0$ and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau=\tau$.
+
+This is the structural reverse of confounding: misclassification attenuates the collider-induced bias toward zero, rather than growing bias from zero toward $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$.

+\begin{proposition}[Collider sign preservation --- proportional case]
+\label{prop:col_prop}
+If $h(A)=\lambda\,g(A)$ for some constant $\lambda$, then $|B(C)|\leq|B(I)|$ for all column-stochastic $C$, and $B(C)$ has the same sign as $B(I)$.
+Consequently, $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau+B_{\mathrm{Berkson}})$.
+\end{proposition}
+
+\begin{proof}
+When $h=\lambda g$, the numerator becomes $-\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])$ and the denominator is $\operatorname{Var}(T)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])$.
+Let $V^*=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])\in[0,V_g]$ where $V_g=\operatorname{Var}(g(A))$.
+Then
+\[
+  |B(C)| = |\lambda|\,\frac{V^*}{\operatorname{Var}(T)-V^*}.
+\]
+The function $x\mapsto x/(a-x)$ is increasing on $[0,a)$.
+With $a=\operatorname{Var}(T)>V_g\geq V^*$:
+$|B(C)|\leq|\lambda|\,V_g/(\operatorname{Var}(T)-V_g)=|B(I)|$.
+At $V^*{=}0$ (uniform $C$): $B{=}0$.  At $V^*{=}V_g$ ($C{=}I$): $B{=}B(I)$.
```

```text
 Five of seven topologies exhibit a structural guarantee: $\tau$-specific sign flip rate equals exactly zero across all sampled confusion matrices (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}). Confounding, mediation, and front-door topologies show 100\% violation and amplification rates but never reverse the sign of the target causal parameter. Collider misclassification attenuates the spurious association induced by conditioning, providing a form of protection. M-bias shows the weakest sensitivity overall (53.2\% violation, 10.4\% amplification) because annotation error on a collider variable reduces, rather than amplifies, the spurious M-bias path.
 
 \paragraph{IV: Wald estimator instability.}
-The instrumental variable topology is the sole exception among non-exposure DAGs. The Wald estimator's first stage involves $C$ directly in the denominator, and 52.6\% of random confusion matrices cause the denominator to change sign, producing $\tau$-specific sign reversal. This rate remains stable across $K{=}3,5,7$ (plan002 cross-$K$ validation), indicating that IV fragility is a structural property of the Wald estimator rather than a finite-$K$ artifact.
+The instrumental variable topology is the sole exception among non-exposure DAGs. The Wald estimator's first stage involves $C$ directly in the denominator, and 52.6\% of random confusion matrices cause the denominator to change sign, producing $\tau$-specific sign reversal. This rate remains stable across $K{=}3,5,7$, indicating that IV fragility is a structural property of the Wald estimator rather than a finite-$K$ artifact.
 
 \paragraph{Cross-$K$ robustness.}
 Experiments at $K{=}5$ and $K{=}7$ (100{,}000 random matrices per topology) confirm that all structural patterns from $K{=}3$ persist. The sign-flip guarantee holds for the same five topologies, and IV sign-flip rates remain within 51--53\%. Worst-case bias magnitudes decrease modestly with $K$ for most topologies but increase for IV at low accuracy (worst case 14.0$\times$ at $K{=}3$ diagonal $\geq 0.5$).

 \subsection{$N$-Sensitivity}
 \label{sec:n_sensitivity}
 
-The significance ASV exhibits a sharp phase transition as a function of sample size. For mediation at $\tau{=}0.05$ with a symmetric mild reference matrix $C_0$: at $N{=}1{,}000$, naive power reaches 0.89 while ASV-filtered power is exactly 0 (the filter blocks all rejections because $1.96 \cdot SE > \tau$). This pattern persists through $N{=}5{,}000$ (naive power 1.0, ASV power 0). At $N{=}10{,}000$, a sharp transition occurs: ASV power jumps to 1.0, matching naive power with zero extra conservatism.
-
-This binary behavior (block-all below $N^*$, pass-all above $N^*$) arises because the significance ASV depends on whether $\tau$ exceeds the critical value $z_{\alpha/2} \cdot SE(N)$. The critical sample size $N^* \approx 6{,}000$--$10{,}000$ for this configuration is analytically computable, providing practitioners with a concrete minimum sample size requirement for their specific topology and effect size.
+The significance ASV exhibits a sharp phase transition: for mediation at $\tau{=}0.05$, ASV-filtered power is exactly 0 for $N{\leq}5{,}000$ (blocking all rejections), then jumps to 1.0 at $N{=}10{,}000$. The critical sample size $N^* \approx 6{,}000$--$10{,}000$ is analytically computable, providing practitioners with a concrete minimum sample size for their topology and effect size.
 
 \subsection{Binarization Collapse}
 \label{sec:binarization}
 
-Collapsing $K{=}3$ annotations to binary introduces additional bias $\Delta = |\text{bias}_{K=3} - \text{bias}_{K \to 2}|$ that varies by 147$\times$ across topologies (Figure~\ref{fig:binarization}). Under 100{,}000 Dirichlet samples, M-bias shows negligible collapse ($\bar{\Delta}{=}0.006$, with 0\% of matrices exceeding $\Delta{>}0.05$), while IV exhibits severe collapse ($\bar{\Delta}{=}0.926$, 100\% exceeding the threshold). Exposure ($\bar{\Delta}{=}0.516$) and IV also introduce sign flips during binarization (14\% and 17.5\% respectively), a phenomenon absent in the other four topologies.
-
-These results demonstrate that binarization is not a safe simplification. For exposure and IV topologies, collapsing multi-class annotations to binary can introduce bias comparable to, or exceeding, the original misclassification bias.
+Collapsing $K{=}3$ annotations to binary introduces additional bias varying 147$\times$ across topologies: from $\bar{\Delta}{=}0.006$ (M-bias) to $\bar{\Delta}{=}0.926$ (IV). Exposure and IV also introduce sign flips during binarization (14\% and 17.5\%), a phenomenon absent in the other topologies.
```
